# CLOSE-OUT LEDGER

**Last updated:** 2026-09-02 (**#254 — TEST MODE, AND ONE FUNCTION THAT ANSWERS "WHICH ORDERS COUNT" FOR ELEVEN READERS.** A business can ring up practice orders all week without one of them reaching its accountant — refused above the invoice POST **and above the customer create**, which is the half people forget. Plus the live `leakage_count` divergence between `api/dashboard.ts` and `Dashboard.tsx`, fixed in the pass that touched it. **Partially delivered against Option C: five of the nine scoped items are NOT built and are named in the row's Blocker column.** 27/27 mutants caught; **5 survived first and every one was a real hole**.) · Prior: #253 · #252 · #251 · #250 · #249.

**Newest: 2026-08-27 (#222 — 🧹 HOUSEKEEPING: A RULING FILED, THREE STORY TAGS CORRECTED, THE TRACKING PASS RUN.** **R-18** filed for the incident log — the ruling is SETTLED, the SHAPE is not, and its first blocker is a **security recon** because the log must be writable by someone whose `auth.uid()` is NULL. 🔴 **The two-month delay is the finding:** the same question was reached and answered silently in `2026-06-27-wrap-and-capture.md` with no ruling and no story, so David's request read as never-built.)

> **STANDING RULE (binding):** This file is the SINGLE close-out record for the platform. Every future build/recon prompt ends by **updating its row here** (bar + SHA + owner-proof status) — not just its own `built-inventory.md` line. `built-inventory.md` answers "was X built?"; THIS ledger answers "is X closed, and if not, what exact live test closes it?" A deliverable whose ledger row is missing or stale is an incomplete task. This is the process fix that stops the tab-pile from re-accumulating.

> **Method:** reconciled against REPO truth — `git log`, `built-inventory.md`, the `verify-universals.mjs` matrix (exit 0, full Cultivar column green), and migration apply-state — NOT editor tabs or memory. Where the handoff text and the code disagreed, the code won (see Fabrication check).

> **The two bars:** **BUILDER-COMPLETE** = committed (SHA), compiles, structural/unit test passes — NOT proven live. **OWNER-PROVEN** = David confirmed live under a real session with a proof artifact (HAR / screenshot / live test). Only OWNER-PROVEN (or doc-only/superseded) closes a tab. **OPEN** = not built, not run, or half-done.

| **#248** | **🔨 BUILD — THE NINETEEN STOPS GET THEIR LINES: A QUICKBOOKS INVOICE BECOMES A HISTORY ORDER, THROUGH A THIRD DOOR AND THE SAME `buildHistoryOrder`.** #246 put nineteen stops on the calendar and **none of them said what was on the truck** — Saturday 5 September showed six stops and nothing said whether that was a full day or a light one. Result: zero-row **85 → 85**, field-lists **35 → 35**, **distinct keys 85 and 35** (so no collision quietly merged two sites into one), and the emitted line-tags reproduce the PREVIOUS baselines exactly — **0 unmatched in either direction**. *A re-key that quietly loses a site is the absorption this fix exists to prevent, arriving through the fix.* **CORRECTION TO THE PROMPT'S FIGURES, stated rather than absorbed: the counts were 85 and 35, not 81 and 37.** **PROBES BOTH DIRECTIONS (STD-022), 8 new:** a comment above a tracked site is NOT new (Z15/F13) · imports and blank lines are NOT new (Z16/F14) · **a genuinely new site still FAILS (Z17/F15)** · a second write on the same table in the same function is a distinct `@2` (Z18) · **renaming the enclosing function DOES re-key it — a real change of identity, stated in the header not hidden (Z19)**. **LIVE PROOF ON THE REAL REPO:** three comment lines inserted at the top of `Dashboard.tsx` — *the exact edit that produced two false NEWs twice yesterday* — and both caps report RATCHET CLEAN. **🔴 TWO SELF-CAUGHT DEFECTS IN THE FIX, both before it was locked in.** **(1)** The first run keyed **38 of 120 sites as `::if#` or `::for#`** — the bare-method alternative matched control-flow keywords, so the key named the wrong thing and hid the function the site is actually in. Found because a routine `grep` for my own new function name returned nothing while `awk` showed it present. One byte; the cap's assertions were unaffected; **the file is text again and searchable.** **THE METHOD RULING, David's, and it names the cause of the whole two-day sequence:** **A STRING IS DERIVED FROM THE ACT, NEVER FROM WHO NEEDS TO PASS.** *"Which string does this person hold"* always produces a plausible answer and only sometimes a correct one — it produced `deliveries:create`, then `orders:update`, **on one line in twelve hours.** *"What capability is being exercised"* produced `orders:create` on the first try. **That is how a gate ends up naming a resource the path does not touch.** Recorded as Lightning's error as much as Thunder's. | `TBD` | **BUILDER-COMPLETE** (`npm run verify` exit 0; **51 capA probes green**; two baselines re-locked for LINE-SHIFT ONLY — **tech-debt #78 firing on cue**, in the same session whose new baseline is keyed `file::binding` to avoid it) | **CARD 16 (NEW):** as the OWNER, `/dashboard` shows an **Inventory value** figure that is not zero and not absent — the #85 fix, and the one surface where a person can see this class of defect without a console. **CARD 17 (NEW):** as the OWNER, `/inventory/import` with a price column mapped does **NOT** say the prices will not save. | #169 | **✅ THE ARCHITECTURAL STANDARD — the platform had 24 verification standards and NO architectural one · A1–A9 · 3 NEW caps · `customers` taken to its end state (phases A–D)** | **THE FINDING, stated plainly because it is the whole session:** `STANDARDS.md` had 24 entries and **every one was REACTIVE** — STD-021 from a wrong corpus, 022 from a detector that could never fail, 023 from a bypassed guard, 024 from a cap that had never run red. **Scars. Not one said how to BUILD a surface.** So **seven write paths to `customers` accumulated with nobody ever deciding to have seven** — each built correctly in isolation, in a session that could not see the others. **The cause is structural, not disciplinary:** Rails/Django/Spring each hand you one form object and one data-access layer and going around them is work; **Supabase supplies neither** — `supabase.from('x').update()` compiles from any component with no wiring and no layer to route through, so **nothing resists a second write path.** **THE STANDARD (`docs/standards/platform-architecture-standard.md`, A1–A9)** — one surface per entity · one write path per table (machine writers included) · one commit model per surface, **the SHAPE decides** · one derived field list · one design system · validation lives with the entity · a gate names a string that exists · **A8 a write that affects zero rows is a failure and says so** · **A9 absent is not empty**. **Each rule states its CHECK or says plainly that it is unenforced, and every rule carries a counter-example from our own code** — it is not aspirational. **THREE are mechanically enforced (A2, A4, A8); A1/A3/A5/A6/A9 are REVIEW-ONLY and the doc calls each a PLACEHOLDER FOR A CAP, not a finished control.** **THE AUDIT** (`platform-architecture-audit-2026-07-29.md`) scores all 33 entities: **80 write paths · 17 violate A2 · 14 clean**, corpus named, derivation stated per column, and a closing section on what it does NOT know. **🔴 "17 failures = 17 DECISIONS owed, not 17 builds" (David, verbatim)** — the sentence that makes the number tractable rather than alarming. Backlog **DEMO-PATH FIRST** (customers → inventory → deliveries → orders → *then* `cost_objects`, the worst number on the board and the one a buyer never opens; rows 1–4 ≈ the work of row 5 alone). **THREE NEW CAPS, each STD-022-probed both directions and STD-024-run failing on the current tree first:** `verify-write-paths` (37 probes; GOAL keeps the debt visible, RATCHET fails only on a NEW path — *a gate that blocks every build gets worked around*), `verify-zero-row-writes` (A8; 11 probes; **84 app sites could not check, exactly ONE could — `api/qbo/router.ts`'s OAuth claim; the one time it was treated as a security boundary it was written correctly, everywhere else as plumbing**), `verify-field-lists` (A4; 12 probes). Plus the **rpc→table map** (tech-debt #76) built AHEAD of every table fix on David's correction — *an ordering ruled against counts known to be a FLOOR is the same error as the number it was ruling on*. **🔴 TWO TABLES NOTHING IN SOURCE REVEALED:** `audit_log` (every writer an RPC) and, one hop deeper, **`business_inventory_ledger`** — written by `emit_inventory_movement`, which **nobody calls from source**, so it was invisible to source AND to the RPC map. It is the ledger D-50's integrity claim rests on. **One hop folded, two NAMED as the bound.** Its five paths are ONE writer on an append-only table (#70): **a declaration, never a merge, and the entry says so.** **Standing assumption: assume a third exists.** **`customers`, PHASES A–D, TO ITS RECORDED END STATE:** 3 surfaces → **1** · 2 commit models → **1** · 6 field lists → **2 declared PROJECTIONS** (A4 now PASSES — *a projection answering one question is not a restated record shape, and forcing a derivation would make the code worse to satisfy a rule aimed elsewhere*) · **A8 on every write site** · **3 write paths, the stated floor**. En route: the form **wrote NOTHING** in edit mode under a footer promising auto-save; **Cancel meant "stop, keeping every write so far"**; `customerUpsert` **NULLED curated addresses from ignorance**; and a partial 8-column row handed to an 18-field editor would have written `'person'` over an organization. **A5: 3 SHARED · 0 HALF · 2 LOCAL** — one shared-modal edit moved TWO surfaces, because C had made the editor shared between them. **🔴 THE HONEST COST — FOUR SELF-CATCHES IN 24 HOURS, ALL BY THE AUTHOR, AND THE FOURTH CLOSES THE ARGUMENT:** E2 recorded a PASS that was not one through a loophole in its own drafting; A4 was scored from a read and **the cap contradicted the audit's own ranking the same day**; two unused exports were shipped into the very file whose header forbids them; **and phase B re-implemented a coercion BESIDE ITS ORIGINAL — covered by §6 r8, a long-standing rule read in that same session — surviving the build, the review and the commit message. Knip caught it, not the rule.** *Knowing a rule does not make you apply it while writing the code the rule is about.* **A rule with no mechanical check will be broken by someone who knows it.** **AND A DEFECT IN THE CAPS THEMSELVES, recorded as its own finding:** `stripComments` collapsed block comments to `''`, shifting every reported line number after one — a real site at `:146` was cited as `:121`. **A CAP WHOSE CITATION IS WRONG IS WORSE THAN ONE THAT SAYS NOTHING: the reader concludes the cap is broken and stops reading it.** Same family as the `[\s\S]*?` near-miss that invented pairings — **both teach distrust of the output, which costs more than a miss does.** Fixed in all three caps; all three baselines re-recorded **with the counts PROVEN FIRST** (zero-row **83 → 81**, two genuinely fixed · field-lists 37 → 37 · write-paths unchanged) — *a silent re-record is indistinguishable from absorbing two.* | `c500513` · `db50189` · `d23b2f6` · `06fe0fd` · `(this commit)` — 2 NEW standards docs · 3 NEW caps + 3 baselines · `customerFieldRegistry.ts` (NEW) · `CustomerPartyEditor` · `customerEdit` · `customerUpsert` · 4 read repoints · `20260729_backfill_billing_from_legacy.sql` · **DELETED** `CustomerEditModal.tsx` + `CustomerFields.tsx` | **BUILDER-COMPLETE · NOT OWNER-PROVEN.** `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new with **four caps chained**; `build:cultivar` exit 0; eslint 248 → 247, baseline re-locked. | **David — `customer-edit-surface-full-surface-test.md`, board 0 of 8.** Card 1 is the write defect head-on (edit → **Save** → **RELOAD**). **Card 7 needs a STAFF session** (holds `customers:read`, NOT `customers:update`) **with a MANAGER positive control in the same sitting** — a refusal message that also fires for the permitted user is a new defect, not a fix. | #168 | **✅ THE ONE CUSTOMER FORM WAS WRITING NOTHING — the tax fold, the certificate shell removed, and E6 phase A (the field registry)** | **THE DEFECT:** `CustomerPartyEditor` coerced every edited field against `draft` — the ON-SCREEN working copy that `input()` had already updated on the keystroke — so `coerceCustomerField`'s unchanged-check asked *"does what I typed equal what I typed?"*, answered yes, and returned without writing. **Fourteen of ~18 fields in EDIT mode silently wrote nothing, under a footer reading "Changes save automatically as you edit."** It hid for two reasons worth recording: the typed value stays on screen until a reload, and the four fields that DID work (`price_tier`/`status`/`credit_limit`/the tax trio) route through `commitPatch`, which never consults the coercion — **the form looked alive because its selects were.** `CustomerEditModal` never had it: it keeps `draft` (persisted) and `form` (working) as two values, which is both why that surface works and how this was diagnosable. **FIXED** by the same split (`saved` vs `draft`), and a `skip` now SNAPS THE INPUT BACK to the persisted value so the screen can never show a value the database does not hold (D-9). **THE TAX GROUP FOLDED IN as an ordinary field group** — the "Save exemption" button removed, every tax control committing like every other group, D-40's invariant (never `tax_exempt=true` without a reason) preserved in ONE writer, `commitExemption`. That fold closed a SECOND lost-data path: a certificate number typed into a group that needed a button the footer said did not exist was silently lost on close. **THE CERTIFICATE UPLOAD SHELL IS REMOVED — the feature is RULED OUT, not deferred** (MASTER_BRIEF PART 1, *TRACE Is Not a Record System*): nothing extracts data from a resale certificate and it is in transit nowhere, so TRACE keeps the reference and the expiry and never the file; the copy now says whose copy is authoritative. **E6 PHASE A — the field registry** (`customerFieldRegistry.ts`): `customers` had SIX parallel field enumerations, none derived from another. Three are now DERIVED from one source — the CORE/FULL select strings (the list most likely to silently omit a field: added to the form, missed in the select, reads back null forever), the create path's own `addText` array, and the notNull/sensitive/mirror sets. **Set-equality proven against every retired list before the swap: CORE 14, FULL 30, TEXT 19, CREATE 12 — all MATCH.** **A REAL HAZARD CAUGHT IN THE BUILD:** the file was first named `customerFields.ts`, which **collides with the `CustomerFields.tsx` COMPONENT on a case-insensitive filesystem** — `./CustomerFields` silently resolved to the new module and broke the build; renamed `customerFieldRegistry.ts` and the reason is in its header. **What is NOT exported is as deliberate as what is:** the text-field derivation is withheld until its consumer exists (phase B), because *an export with no consumer is a claim that a consolidation happened when it has not*. | `(this commit)` — `scripts/verify-write-paths.mjs` · `write-paths-baseline.json` · `customerFieldRegistry.ts` (NEW) · `CustomerPartyEditor.tsx` · `customerEdit.ts` · `Customers.tsx` · `supabase/migrations/20260729_backfill_billing_from_legacy.sql` (NEW — the REPO RECORD of David's live-applied backfill, 15→0 legacy-only, 3→0 orgs) · `ui-control-standards.md` (**NEW §4**) · `MASTER_BRIEF.md` · `user_stories.md` · `COST-TO-PRODUCE-DESIGN.md` | **BUILDER-COMPLETE · NOT OWNER-PROVEN.** `build:cultivar` exit 0; `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new. | **David — `customer-edit-surface-full-surface-test.md`, board 0 of 7.** Card 1 is the defect head-on: edit an email, blur, close, **RELOAD**, re-open — reload is the whole point, because before the fix the value stayed on screen and was never written. Card 3 proves the tax fold saves with no button. Cards 5–6 are the negatives (exempt-without-reason refused; blanked first name snaps back). | **🔴 CARD 7 IS `needs-test`, NOT `owed`, because the behaviour it checks is NOT FIXED:** no write on this record checks affected rows, and a PostgREST UPDATE matching zero rows returns NO error — so a save by a caller RLS refuses reports success and writes nothing. Latent under owner-only RLS today; live the moment a non-owner gets a write path. **E5 is a ruling David owes.** Merge continues at phase B (one commit model) → C (absorb `CustomerEditModal`) → D (**UNGATED** by the backfill). Realistic floor is **3 paths, not 1**. |
| #167 | **✅ THE WRITE-PATH CAP — "more than one write path to a table fails the build unless declared" · `scripts/verify-write-paths.mjs` (NEW) · RATCHETED and CHAINED into `npm run verify`** | **THE ROOT CAUSE, named by David and recorded because it explains the whole class:** when he wrote CRUD by hand, a second surface cost him time, so he built one. For a model, **generating a new component is CHEAPER than finding and reusing the existing one** — reuse requires reading and understanding what is already there first. So write paths to a table accumulated, each locally sensible, each shipped in a session that could not see the others; `customers` reached **five in app code alone** (seven counting logical surfaces). **Nothing in the loop ever asked "does this record already have a write path?"** This cap asks it mechanically. **A PATH IS A FILE, not a call site** — `inventoryEdit.ts` writes `business_inventory` at five call sites and is ONE path; that is the shape being protected (§6 r8 / STD-011). **TWO VERDICTS, REPORTED TOGETHER AND DELIBERATELY SO:** *GOAL* (one path per table) stays informational so the known debt remains **visible** rather than becoming invisible; *RATCHET* (no NEW undeclared path vs `write-paths-baseline.json`) is the build-failing assertion, the same zero-net-new shape `npm run verify` already uses for tsc/eslint/knip. **David's ruling on why: a gate that blocks every build gets worked around, and a worked-around gate is worse than none** — the ratchet makes surface EIGHT impossible tomorrow without waiting for the seven to be resolved, and it was built BEFORE the first table was fixed so the baseline could be captured while the count was still still. **FIRST RUN, on the current tree, failing, before any fix (STD-024): 17 tables FAIL, 13 PASS.** `business_members` 6 · `businesses` 6 · `cost_objects` 6 · `customers` 5 · `member_devices` 4 · `business_inventory` 3 · `orders` 3 · ten more at 2. **🔴 "17 failures = 17 DECISIONS owed, not 17 builds" (David, verbatim) — it is what makes the number tractable rather than alarming, and it is the difference between a finding and a backlog.** Many resolve to a DECLARATION (`ALLOWED_DIVERGENCE`, with its reason) rather than a merge — `business_members`' six are lifecycle stages, and `business_accounting_secrets`' two write **disjoint column sets**. **EVERY COUNT IS A FLOOR, NOT A TOTAL, and the report says so on every run:** the cap reads SOURCE, so an RPC's target table — which lives in the DATABASE — is invisible to it. **11 RPC writers and 12 unresolved dynamic table names** (incl. `syncEngine`'s `op.table`, a generic writer that can target anything) are REPORTED and not judged. | #165 | **#74 RULED AND BUILT — THE FUNNEL SHORT-CIRCUITS A NO-OP SAVE AND STILL RECORDS THE ACT · STD-023 ADDED (the general form of the gate failure) · owner-test card 8** · migration `20260728c_funnel_no_op_short_circuit.sql` (**NEW, GATED**) · zero app code, zero api-fn (12/12) | **DAVID'S RULING, IMPLEMENTED LITERALLY:** short-circuit the write — no `role_definitions` update, no member re-materialization, return `applied=false, reason='no-op'` — **but KEEP the audit row, with `outcome='no_change'` and both before/after recorded.** His reasoning is the load-bearing part and is preserved in the migration header: *a Save that changed nothing is an OPERATOR ACT on a permissions surface — someone opened Roles, looked at a role, pressed Save, and that is worth keeping. What is not acceptable is `outcome='success'` on `action='role.permissions_changed'` when nothing changed, **because the action name asserts an event that did not occur.*** `no_change` rows are ignorable by anything counting changes and present for anything reconstructing who touched what — **silence loses the second and gains nothing on the first.** **🔴 SETS, NOT ARRAYS (David's correction, and it would have reintroduced the defect from the other side):** jsonb array ordering is not guaranteed stable across a re-materialization, so `before <> after` on raw arrays reports a change on a **pure REORDERING** — a false positive that makes the fix itself lie. Implemented as two-way jsonb containment (order- and duplicate-insensitive by definition); **V3 is the check that tells a set implementation from an array one** — same elements, reversed order, must still return `no-op`. **FOUR CASES THAT ARE NOT NO-OPS, each in the migration with its reason:** ① **NO TENANT ROW YET — saving a set identical to the floor MINTS the tenant override, the ONE-WAY DOOR.** Identical contents, profoundly different state; short-circuiting it would make the most consequential write in the system the most silent one. ② a **label/description** edit with an identical permission set is a real edit — *a short-circuit that swallowed it would be a new defect shipped inside the fix for an old one.* ③ a **DRIFTED member array** — the service-key/postgres path is still open by necessity (#152), so drift is reachable, and a save that repairs it changed something. **⚠️ THIS ONE IS AN INTERPRETATION AND IS FLAGGED AS SUCH: the ruling says "compares desired vs current resolved," which does not address a drifted member. Resolved in the direction that keeps `no_change` truthful — David's to correct.** ④ `p_op <> 'save'`; `reset`/`create`/`delete` deliberately out of scope (a `reset` matching zero rows is arguably a no-op too, but its resolved-after can legitimately differ, so it is left alone rather than guessed at). **THE STATED COST, because an unstated cost is a trap:** `action='role.permissions_changed'` alone now includes no-ops — **anything counting changes must filter on `outcome`** — recorded in the migration header, in the `COMMENT ON COLUMN audit_log.outcome` (which named two values and now names three), and on the card. **STD-023 — the general form, and it belongs in STANDARDS because the defect is not about permissions:** *a guard the write does not depend on is advice, not a gate.* Twin of STD-022 one layer out — STD-022 asks *can this check ever fail?*, STD-023 asks *can the operation ever proceed without this check?* **With the corollary found in the fix: PLANNER-DEPENDENT SAFETY IS NOT SAFETY** — expressing the premise as the DRIVING RELATION rather than a trailing `WHERE … EXISTS` makes *"no rows ⇒ not called"* a property of the query SHAPE, not a planner decision about when to push a qual below a lateral join against a VOLATILE function. Applied three ways: runbooks wrap the guarded write in an explicit `BEGIN … COMMIT` **and** put the premise in the writing statement (two mechanisms, because one was proven a single point of failure); a precondition belongs INSIDE the writing function, not in every caller (*a rule enforced by each caller remembering it is enforced by memory*); and the review test — *"what is the shortest path by which this write executes and this check does not run?"* **✅ APPLIED AND VERIFIED 2026-07-28 (David, at postgres) — all four probes.** V1 no-op → `applied=false, reason='no-op', outcome='no_change'`, 40 → 40 · **🔴 V3 REORDER — the same 40 elements in REVERSED order → `applied=false, reason='no-op'`. SET COMPARISON CONFIRMED.** This is the only probe that distinguishes this build from an array-comparison one: an array build returns `applied=true` here **and passes every other check**, which is precisely why it was written · V4 label-change → `applied=true`, `member_id` populated (a label edit with an unchanged permission set is not a no-op, as ruled), rolled back, nothing persisted · source check: `prosrc` contains the short-circuit. **🔴 THE AUDIT LOG IS NOW ITS OWN BEFORE/AFTER OF THE FIX, AND THE PROBE ROWS ARE NOT TO BE PRUNED:** `18:13:29 rbac-cleanup:assets-retired · success · 40 → 40` (the defect) → `18:45:08 v1-noop-probe · no_change · 40 → 40` (fixed) → `18:45:25 v3-reorder-probe · no_change · 40 → 40` (fixed, and order-insensitive). Three rows, same tenant, same role, same counts — **the only difference is `outcome`, which is the entire property this migration exists to establish.** A future audit-log cleanup removing "probe noise" would delete the demonstration that the guard is alive; that is STD-022's concern applied to the EVIDENCE rather than to the check. | `(this commit)` — `supabase/migrations/20260728c_funnel_no_op_short_circuit.sql` (NEW, **APPLIED**) · `STANDARDS.md` (**STD-023**, v2.6) · `docs/owner-tests/team-permissions-full-surface-test.md` (**NEW card 8**, board 7 → 8 cards) · tech-debt **#74 ✅ RESOLVED** | **BUILDER-COMPLETE · DEPLOYED · MIGRATION APPLIED AND VERIFIED · SCREEN HALF NOT OWNER-PROVEN.** `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new. So a pure non-override order omitted it on every row → default applied → OK (Friday); an order with ONE override injected the column → the non-override rows NULL-violated → 500. **NOT a D-39 regression** — `git blame` shows this region last touched by `1c13d46` (2026-07-08 leakage build); D-39 (`8f53698`) never touched `order_service_selections`/`overrideCols`. It surfaced now only because the 20260708 migration is applied AND a service override was exercised for the first time. **FIX:** `overrideCols` now returns `{ is_manual_override: false }` for non-override rows (truthful per-row — true on the overridden line, false on the others; every batch row carries the key → no NULL). Column already has `DEFAULT false` → **NO migration needed**. Discount/pricing logic untouched (write-constraint fix only). New `[TRACE:LEAKAGE]` logs per-row is_manual_override + override count on the write. ONE file. (2) crisp 1px freeze line (`1px 0 0 0 #d1d5db`) + soft depth shadow on the last frozen col. (3) scroll box bleeds flush to the card edges (`margin:0 -1.25rem`) reclaiming the left gutter. | 59 | Grower resolve design — format-agnostic scan→catalog (FALSE-UNKNOWN root cause) | READ-ONLY churn of the full `data/grower-scan/` corpus (28 catalogs, **1,382 rows**, 8 with real catalogs). **Root cause of the LAWNS FALSE-UNKNOWN:** the resolver joins on `tag_id`/`sku` only, but discovery writes synthetic `DISC-####` skus + no `cultivar_plants` row, so a QR product-slug shares **zero chars** with the catalog except the name's words — which are never compared. **Identifier distribution (the decider): per-item URL/slug = 12.4% (171 rows, 3 growers); name-only = 87.6%; real POS SKU = 0%.** So slug-as-key is impossible as the base. **Canonical key = normalized NAME token-set (common ∪ botanical)** — present ~100%, **empirically resolves ~97%** of slug-bearing rows (93=96% / ATX=100% / Calloway's=95%, 167/172) with **~0 false collisions** across 1,258 rows (token-set EQUALITY alone fixes LAWNS' word-order flip). **Layered resolver:** tag_id → sku → stored-slug-exact → token-set equality → guarded subset (>1 candidate ⇒ NEED_CLARIFICATION, never silent) → stemmed → UNKNOWN. **Discovery-capture change (recommend, not built):** `catalog.ts` already harvests per-product hrefs (`:79`) then discards them → carry `sourceSlug` onto `CatalogItem` + store a `source_slug` matchable column in `populate.ts` (today buried in free-text `notes`); gated migration ⇒ schema gate applies, flagged not taken. **Landmines evidenced:** ®/™ + quotes + parens + `&`→`amp` entity leak, cultivar collisions (7× Cercis Redbuds — keep ALL tokens, never genus+common), source typos, plural/possessive, name≠slug, 87.6% no-identifier growers. **No item claimed done that the code contradicts** (the one mismatch — `__PENDING__` SHAs — was the handoff lagging the commits, corrected above).

### 🔴 GENERAL FINDING 2026-07-27 — AN ARTIFACT CONFIDENTLY NAMING THINGS THAT ARE NOT THERE

Three instances in one week, and they are ONE SHAPE, not three unrelated slips. Recorded here as a
GENERAL finding rather than filed under the build that surfaced each — the pricing one is the
third symptom, not the disease.

| # | artifact | named | reality |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | build-plan §2's legacy-permission inventory | a complete list of legacy strings | built from CODE, never checked against DATA — missed `process_orders`, `manage_team`, both live in member arrays |
| 2 | `verify-financial-permissions.mjs` | a captured baseline of member rows | asserts against business ids that **do not exist** |
| 3 | spec §5's protected-recipe fields | `baselineMargin`, `referencePrice`, `markup` | 3 of 4 do not exist on the thing being protected; `markup` exists nowhere in the vertical |

**THE COMMON MECHANISM: the artifact was written from ANOTHER ARTIFACT rather than from the thing
it describes.** #3 is the clearest — `baselineMargin` and `priceReference` are real fields on
`CostToProduceResult`, the COMPUTED OUTPUT, while the thing being guarded is
`CostToProduceConfig`, the STORED INPUT. **A guard written from the shape of the ANSWER rather
than the shape of the thing being guarded.** `markup` came from a *different vertical's* pricing
model.

**WHY IT SURVIVES REVIEW:** every one of these reads as correct. A list of plausible field names,
a baseline of plausible ids, an inventory of plausible strings. Nothing looks wrong, and the
checks built on top of them **report PASS** — `config ? 'baselineMargin'` returned false whether or
not the recipe had been damaged. **A guard that cannot fail is not a guard**, and it is
indistinguishable from a working one on the board.

**THE MECHANICAL ANSWER, and it is why three caps landed this week:** an assertion must be written
from — and must READ — the thing it asserts about.
- **STD-021** covers the CLAIM half: a negative claim names its corpus and its method.
- **STD-022** covers the RUNNING half: an assertion ships with a planted-bad probe it must reject
  and a planted-good probe it must accept.
- **capC** is the shape applied: it reads the TYPE and fails on any unclassified field, so it
  cannot name a field that is not there and cannot miss one that is.
- **capQ (d)** and **capK** are the same move for the floor and for the api layer.

**The open question this leaves:** every one of the three was found by David reading output, not by
a check. The caps close these three shapes; they do not close the class. Assume the fourth instance
exists and is currently green.

Reconciling the two into one table is a docs-hygiene follow-up, deliberately not bundled into a build (unchanged from the 2026-07-23 note below — the split is real and still owed).
